  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "utsushihikanasaku-c01",
      "source_id": "utsushihikanasaku-s1",
      "quote": "宇都志日金拆命（うつしひかなさくのみこと）は、日本神話に登場する神。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Utsushihikanasaku adalah dewa Jepang.",
        "en": "Utsushihikanasaku is a Japanese deity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "utsushihikanasaku-c02",
      "source_id": "utsushihikanasaku-s1",
      "quote": "『古事記』では宇都志日金拆命と表記し、その他の史料では穂高見命（ほだかみのみこと）の名で伝わる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Catatan lain menyebutnya Hotakami.",
        "en": "Other records call him Hotakami."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "utsushihikanasaku-c03",
      "source_id": "utsushihikanasaku-s1",
      "quote": "『古事記』に登場する海の国津神で、伊邪那岐命が黄泉国から帰ってきた後、日向の瀬で禊を行った際に生まれた綿津見神たちの子で、安曇氏の祖神と伝わる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kojiki menjadikannya dewa laut keturunan Watatsumi dan leluhur klan Azumi.",
        "en": "The Kojiki makes him a sea deity descended from Watatsumi and an ancestor of the Azumi clan."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "utsushihikanasaku-c04",
      "source_id": "utsushihikanasaku-s1",
      "quote": "佐久郡の地名由来として、宇都志日金拆命が開拓したので「拆（サク）」となったという説がある。 また、穂高岳に降臨し、信濃国の安曇氏の祖となったともされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi daerah menyebutnya membuka Saku dan turun ke Gunung Hotaka.",
        "en": "Regional traditions credit him with developing Saku and descending on Mount Hotaka."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Wakka-us Kamuy adalah dewi air tawar Ainu.",
    "en": "Wakka-us Kamuy is the Ainu goddess of freshwater.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wakka-us-kamuy-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Wakka-us Kamuy adalah dewi air tawar Ainu. Petorush Mat adalah nama lain, dimaknai sebagai perempuan tempat air. Ia digambarkan berambut panjang serta mahir menari dan menyanyi.",
      "en": "Wakka-us Kamuy is the Ainu goddess of freshwater. Petorush Mat is another name, glossed as Watering-place Woman. She is depicted with long hair and skill in dancing and singing.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wakka-us-kamuy-c01",
        "wakka-us-kamuy-c02",
        "wakka-us-kamuy-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Manusia meminta perantaraannya kepada kami lain. Dalam kisah kelaparan ia dan Hasinaw-uk Kamuy melepaskan rusa serta salmon yang ditahan. Ia memperingatkan Okikurmi dalam mimpi untuk menjaga ritual yang tepat.",
      "en": "People ask her to intercede with other kamuy. In a famine story she and Hasinaw-uk Kamuy release the withheld deer and salmon. She warns Okikurmi in a dream to uphold the proper rituals.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wakka-us-kamuy-c04",
        "wakka-us-kamuy-c05",
        "wakka-us-kamuy-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wakka-us-kamuy-c01",
      "source_id": "wakka-us-kamuy-s1",
      "quote": "Wakka-us Kamuy (Ainu ワッカウシカムイ) is the Ainu kamuy (goddess) of fresh water.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wakka-us Kamuy adalah dewi air tawar Ainu.",
        "en": "Wakka-us Kamuy is the Ainu goddess of freshwater."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wakka-us-kamuy-c02",
      "source_id": "wakka-us-kamuy-s1",
      "quote": "She is also known as Petorush Mat (Watering-place Woman).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Petorush Mat adalah nama lain, dimaknai sebagai perempuan tempat air.",
        "en": "Petorush Mat is another name, glossed as Watering-place Woman."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wakka-us-kamuy-c03",
      "source_id": "wakka-us-kamuy-s1",
      "quote": "Wakka-us Kamuy is portrayed as a long-haired woman who is a skilled dancer and singer.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia digambarkan berambut panjang serta mahir menari dan menyanyi.",
        "en": "She is depicted with long hair and skill in dancing and singing."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wakka-us-kamuy-c04",
      "source_id": "wakka-us-kamuy-s1",
      "quote": "She is sympathetic to humanity, and is sometimes petitioned to intercede with other kamuy on their behalf.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Manusia meminta perantaraannya kepada kami lain.",
        "en": "People ask her to intercede with other kamuy."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wakka-us-kamuy-c05",
      "source_id": "wakka-us-kamuy-s1",
      "quote": "Wakka-us Kamuy and the sympathetic Hasinaw-uk Kamuy, while continuing to dance, send their souls to the storehouses and let the deer and salmon loose; in order to avoid making a scene, the other kamuy had no choice but to continue the feast.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah kelaparan ia dan Hasinaw-uk Kamuy melepaskan rusa serta salmon yang ditahan.",
        "en": "In a famine story she and Hasinaw-uk Kamuy release the withheld deer and salmon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wakka-us-kamuy-c06",
      "source_id": "wakka-us-kamuy-s1",
      "quote": "Afterward, Wakka-us Kamuy sends a dream to Okikurmi, telling him what had happened and why, and warning him to see that the rituals were carried out in proper fashion.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia memperingatkan Okikurmi dalam mimpi untuk menjaga ritual yang tepat.",
        "en": "She warns Okikurmi in a dream to uphold the proper rituals."
      }
    }
  ],

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Warai-onna adalah yōkai tertawa dari Kōchi. Tradisi memperingatkan pertemuan pada hari pertama, kesembilan, dan ketujuh belas setiap bulan. Pertemuan samurai di gunung menampilkan perempuan muda yang tawanya memenuhi sekitar.",
      "en": "Warai-onna is a laughing yōkai of Kōchi. Tradition warns of encounters on the first, ninth and seventeenth days of each month. A samurai’s mountain encounter features a young woman whose laughter fills the surroundings.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "warai-onna-c01",
        "warai-onna-c02",
        "warai-onna-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Nanroshi menceritakan kisah yang sama dengan remaja laki-laki alih-alih perempuan. Uraian belakangan juga mengenali warai otoko laki-laki dengan perilaku serupa. Penuturan belakangan menyebut tawa itu menetap pada Kandayū sampai meninggal.",
      "en": "Nanroshi tells the same story with a teenage boy rather than a woman. The later account also recognizes male warai otoko with similar behavior. The later telling has the laughter remain with Kandayū until death.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "warai-onna-c04",
        "warai-onna-c05",
        "warai-onna-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "statement": {
        "id": "Warai-onna adalah yōkai tertawa dari Kōchi.",
        "en": "Warai-onna is a laughing yōkai of Kōchi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "warai-onna-c02",
      "source_id": "warai-onna-s1",
      "quote": "毎月1日、9日、17日に山に入るとこれに遭い、半死半生になってしまうといわれた。「勝賀瀬の赤頭」「本山の白姥」と並び、土佐の3大妖魔の一つとされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi memperingatkan pertemuan pada hari pertama, kesembilan, dan ketujuh belas setiap bulan.",
        "en": "Tradition warns of encounters on the first, ninth and seventeenth days of each month."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "warai-onna-c03",
      "source_id": "warai-onna-s1",
      "quote": "あるとき、樋口関太夫という者がこの言い伝えを無視し、家来たちを引き連れて山に入ったところ、17,18歳程度の女性が関太夫を指差して笑っていた。次第に笑い声が高くなり、周りの石、植物、水、風までもが大笑いしているように笑い声が轟いた。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pertemuan samurai di gunung menampilkan perempuan muda yang tawanya memenuhi sekitar.",
        "en": "A samurai’s mountain encounter features a young woman whose laughter fills the surroundings."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "warai-onna-c04",
      "source_id": "warai-onna-s1",
      "quote": "なお、文化時代の土佐の地誌『南路志』に、これとまったく同じ物語があるが、題は「笑い男（わらいおとこ）」であり、登場する妖怪は女性ではなく、十代半ばの少年とされている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nanroshi menceritakan kisah yang sama dengan remaja laki-laki alih-alih perempuan.",
        "en": "Nanroshi tells the same story with a teenage boy rather than a woman."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "warai-onna-c05",
      "source_id": "warai-onna-s2",
      "quote": "Legends also tell of warai otoko—male versions of the same yōkai. They behave exactly the same way as warai onna.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian belakangan juga mengenali warai otoko laki-laki dengan perilaku serupa.",
        "en": "The later account also recognizes male warai otoko with similar behavior."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "warai-onna-c06",
      "source_id": "warai-onna-s2",
      "quote": "Although he escaped successfully, Kandayū was haunted by that laughter, which echoed in his ears until he died.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penuturan belakangan menyebut tawa itu menetap pada Kandayū sampai meninggal.",
        "en": "The later telling has the laughter remain with Kandayū until death."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
    {
      "topic": {
        "id": "Wujud pada versi kisah / Figure in story versions",
        "en": "Wujud pada versi kisah / Figure in story versions"
      },
      "positions": [
        {
          "summary": {
            "id": "Perempuan muda dalam kisah Warai-onna.",
            "en": "A young woman in the Warai-onna account."
          },
          "claim_ids": [
            "warai-onna-c01",
            "warai-onna-c03"
          ]
        },
        {
          "summary": {
            "id": "Remaja laki-laki dalam versi Warai-otoko.",
            "en": "A teenage boy in the Warai-otoko version."
          },
          "claim_ids": [
            "warai-onna-c04",
            "warai-onna-c05"
          ]
        }
      ]
    }
  ],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Belum ada berkas Wikimedia Commons yang diperiksa untuk entri ini."
    }
  ]
}

  "short_description": {

    "en": "Yūrei-danuki is a transforming tanuki of Inoshiri in Tokushima.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Yūrei-danuki adalah tanuki pengubah wujud dari Inoshiri di Tokushima. Ia menempati pohon pemakaman tempat Hyōhachi yang pemberani menunggu untuk mengungkapnya.",
      "en": "Yūrei-danuki is a transforming tanuki of Inoshiri in Tokushima. It occupies a cemetery tree where the brave Hyōhachi waits to expose it.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q10282467-c01",
        "wd-q10282467-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia menghasilkan penampakan ibu Hyōhachi yang dipukulnya dengan kapak. Pada fajar dongeng memperlihatkan bangkai tanuki sangat tua.",
      "en": "It produces an apparition of Hyōhachi’s mother, which he strikes with an axe. At dawn the tale reveals the corpse of an extremely old tanuki.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q10282467-c03",
        "wd-q10282467-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],

    {
      "id": "wd-q10282467-s1",
      "published": null,

      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E5%B9%BD%E9%9C%8A%E7%8B%B8",

      "publisher": "Wikipedia",

    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q10282467-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q10282467-s1",
      "quote": "幽霊狸（ゆうれいだぬき）は、阿波国美馬郡脇町の猪尻村（現・徳島県美馬市）に伝わる化け狸。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Yūrei-danuki adalah tanuki pengubah wujud dari Inoshiri di Tokushima.",
        "en": "Yūrei-danuki is a transforming tanuki of Inoshiri in Tokushima."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10282467-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q10282467-s1",
      "quote": "猪尻村の樽井という地の墓場にエノキの大木があり、幽霊狸はこの木の根元を巣としていた。あるときに猪尻村に住む兵八という豪胆者が狸の正体を見抜いてやろうと、斧を手にして出かけ、誰も近寄らなかった大木に登って夜を待った。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menempati pohon pemakaman tempat Hyōhachi yang pemberani menunggu untuk mengungkapnya.",
        "en": "It occupies a cemetery tree where the brave Hyōhachi waits to expose it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10282467-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q10282467-s1",
      "quote": "兵八はさすがに心細くなり、母は本当に死んでしまったのかと下を見下ろすと、棺桶を埋めた地面が盛り上がって母親の幽霊が飛び出し、「親不孝者め、殺してやる!」と襲って来た。兵八は咄嗟に斧を振り上げ、幽霊の頭目掛けて叩きつけた。悲鳴が響き、幽霊は地面へと消えた。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menghasilkan penampakan ibu Hyōhachi yang dipukulnya dengan kapak.",
        "en": "It produces an apparition of Hyōhachi’s mother, which he strikes with an axe."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10282467-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q10282467-s1",
      "quote": "やがて夜が明け、地面には数百歳か想像もつかなほどの古狸、幽霊狸の死体が転がっていた。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada fajar dongeng memperlihatkan bangkai tanuki sangat tua.",
        "en": "At dawn the tale reveals the corpse of an extremely old tanuki."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Fuguruma Yōhi adalah yōkai yang digambar Sekien.",
    "en": "Fuguruma Yōhi is a yōkai illustrated by Sekien.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q10301256-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Fuguruma Yōhi adalah yōkai yang digambar Sekien. Satu tafsir menyebutnya lahir dari keterikatan yang menumpuk pada surat cinta tua. Peneliti menghubungkan namanya serta Chirizuka Kaiō dengan esai 72 Tsurezuregusa.",
      "en": "Fuguruma Yōhi is a yōkai illustrated by Sekien. An interpretation makes her arise from attachment accumulated in old love letters. Researchers link her name and Chirizuka Kaiō with essay 72 of Tsurezuregusa.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q10301256-c01",
        "wd-q10301256-c02",
        "wd-q10301256-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Gulungan belakangan menyebut sosok salinannya Bunshō no Kai. Uraian belakangan memberi roh itu penampilan perempuan mirip raksasa dan pakaian compang-camping. Uraian belakangan juga menghubungkan kelahirannya dengan emosi dalam surat cinta tersimpan.",
      "en": "A later scroll calls the copied figure Bunshō no Kai. The later description gives the spirit an ogrelike female appearance and tattered clothes. The later account also connects her birth with emotion in stored love letters.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q10301256-c04",
        "wd-q10301256-c05",
        "wd-q10301256-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],
  "relations": [],
  "modern_depictions": [],
  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q10301256-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",

      "title": "文車妖妃",

      "author": null
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10301256-s2",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://yokai.com/fugurumayouhi/",
      "title": "Fuguruma yōhi",
      "publisher": "Yokai.com",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "author": "Matthew Meyer"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q10301256-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q10301256-s1",
      "quote": "文車妖妃（ふぐるまようひ、ふぐるまようび）は、鳥山石燕の妖怪画集『百器徒然袋』にある日本の妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Fuguruma Yōhi adalah yōkai yang digambar Sekien.",
        "en": "Fuguruma Yōhi is a yōkai illustrated by Sekien."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10301256-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q10301256-s1",
      "quote": "石燕は「執着の思ひをこめし千束の玉章（たまづさ）にはかかるあやしきかたちをもあらはしぬべしと夢の中に思ひぬ」と記しており、古い恋文が変化した・恋文につもった執念がなったものであると解釈されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu tafsir menyebutnya lahir dari keterikatan yang menumpuk pada surat cinta tua.",
        "en": "An interpretation makes her arise from attachment accumulated in old love letters."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10301256-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q10301256-s1",
      "quote": "石燕は『百器徒然袋』の妖怪に付けている詞書きに『徒然草』の文章を多く引いており、『徒然草』第72段の「多くて見苦しからぬは、文車の文、塵塚の塵」という一文があり、この「文車の文」から文車妖妃、「塵塚の塵」から塵塚怪王が創作されたと考えられている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Peneliti menghubungkan namanya serta Chirizuka Kaiō dengan esai 72 Tsurezuregusa.",
        "en": "Researchers link her name and Chirizuka Kaiō with essay 72 of Tsurezuregusa."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10301256-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q10301256-s1",
      "quote": "作者不詳の『妖怪絵巻』（東洋大学附属図書館 所蔵）には石燕からの摸写と考えられる作例として文章怪（ぶんしょうのかい）という名で絵が描かれている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gulungan belakangan menyebut sosok salinannya Bunshō no Kai.",
        "en": "A later scroll calls the copied figure Bunshō no Kai."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10301256-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q10301256-s2",
      "quote": "Fuguruma yōhi is a spirit which resembles and ogreish human woman in tattered clothing.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian belakangan memberi roh itu penampilan perempuan mirip raksasa dan pakaian compang-camping.",
        "en": "The later description gives the spirit an ogrelike female appearance and tattered clothes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10301256-c06",
      "source_id": "wd-q10301256-s2",
      "quote": "In particular, it is the emotion and attachment built up in the piles of love letters stored in these carts which gives birth to this yōkai.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian belakangan juga menghubungkan kelahirannya dengan emosi dalam surat cinta tersimpan.",
        "en": "The later account also connects her birth with emotion in stored love letters."
      }
    }
  ],

      "id": "wd-q10329896-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q10329896-s1",
      "quote": "『山海経』の「西山経」には、小次山に棲んでおり、猿に似た姿で、首は白く脚は赤いと記されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kitab Gunung dan Laut memberinya leher putih serta kaki merah di Gunung Xiaoci.",
        "en": "The Classic of Mountains and Seas gives it a white neck and red feet at Mount Xiaoci."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10329896-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q10329896-s1",
      "quote": "郭璞は「山海経図讃」で「鳧徯朱厭、見則有兵」と戦乱の起こるきざしとされる鳥獣として朱厭の名をピックアップしている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Guo Pu juga menyebutnya makhluk penanda peperangan.",
        "en": "Guo Pu also cites it as a creature signaling conflict."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Kaum Qizhong adalah bangsa utara legendaris dalam tradisi Tiongkok.",
    "en": "Qizhong people are a legendary northern race in Chinese tradition.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q10542923-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kaum Qizhong adalah bangsa utara legendaris dalam tradisi Tiongkok. Kitab Gunung dan Laut menempatkan negeri mereka di timur kaum Juying dan menggambarkan kaki besar.",
      "en": "Qizhong people are a legendary northern race in Chinese tradition. The Classic of Mountains and Seas places their land east of the Juying people and describes huge feet.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q10542923-c01",
        "wd-q10542923-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Komentar menyebut mereka berjalan tanpa menempatkan tumit di tanah. Novel Bunga dalam Cermin menggambarkan mereka berjalan dengan ujung jari kaki.",
      "en": "Commentary says they walk without placing heels on the ground. The novel Flowers in the Mirror portrays them walking on their toes.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q10542923-c03",
        "wd-q10542923-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,

      "id": "wd-q10542923-s1",
      "published": null,

      "title": "跂踵人",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q10542923-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q10542923-s1",
      "quote": "跂踵人（きしょうじん）は中国に伝わる伝説上の人種である。大踵とも。古代中国では北方に位置する国に棲んでいたとされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kaum Qizhong adalah bangsa utara legendaris dalam tradisi Tiongkok.",
        "en": "Qizhong people are a legendary northern race in Chinese tradition."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10542923-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q10542923-s1",
      "quote": "古代中国の地理書『山海経』の海外北経によると、跂踵国は拘纓国の東にあり、跂踵人は人間の姿をしているがとても大きく、特に足が大きいという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kitab Gunung dan Laut menempatkan negeri mereka di timur kaum Juying dan menggambarkan kaki besar.",
        "en": "The Classic of Mountains and Seas places their land east of the Juying people and describes huge feet."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10542923-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q10542923-s1",
      "quote": "『淮南子』の注などによると、跂踵人たちは決して踵（かかと）を地面につけずに歩くとも伝えられており、爪先だけで立って居る姿を描いた『山海経』の図も確認することが出来る。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Komentar menyebut mereka berjalan tanpa menempatkan tumit di tanah.",
        "en": "Commentary says they walk without placing heels on the ground."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10542923-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q10542923-s1",
      "quote": "『鏡花縁』 跂踵国が旅の途中に舞台として登場する。跂踵人はぶあつい足をしており、爪先だけでたって歩くとされる。主人公たちの一行は結局この歩き方を遠くから見ただけで上陸はしなかった。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Novel Bunga dalam Cermin menggambarkan mereka berjalan dengan ujung jari kaki.",
        "en": "The novel Flowers in the Mirror portrays them walking on their toes."
      }
    }
  ],

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Feitouman adalah sosok gaib Tiongkok yang kepalanya lepas dan terbang pada malam hari.",
    "en": "Feitouman is a Chinese supernatural figure whose head detaches and flies at night.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q10566254-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Feitouman adalah sosok gaib Tiongkok yang kepalanya lepas dan terbang pada malam hari. Sancai Tuhui menggambarkan manusia penerbang kepala di Jawa dengan mata tanpa pupil.",
      "en": "Feitouman is a Chinese supernatural figure whose head detaches and flies at night. Sancai Tuhui describes head-flying people in Java with eyes lacking pupils.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q10566254-c01",
        "wd-q10566254-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kisah selatan memberi mereka tanda merah di leher, telinga mirip sayap, dan kepulangan pada fajar. Kisah menyebut penutupan tubuh dapat menghalangi kembalinya kepala dan menyebabkan kematian.",
      "en": "A southern account gives them red neck marks, winglike ears and a dawn return. Stories say covering the body can obstruct the head’s return and cause death.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q10566254-c03",
        "wd-q10566254-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],
  "relations": [],
  "modern_depictions": [],
  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q10566254-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E9%A3%9B%E9%A0%AD%E8%9B%AE",
      "title": "飛頭蛮",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q10566254-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q10566254-s1",
      "quote": "飛頭蛮（ひとうばん）は、中国の妖怪。古典の記述によれば、通常は人間の姿と変わりないが、夜になると首（頭部）だけが胴体から離れて空中を飛び回るものとされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Feitouman adalah sosok gaib Tiongkok yang kepalanya lepas dan terbang pada malam hari.",
        "en": "Feitouman is a Chinese supernatural figure whose head detaches and flies at night."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10566254-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q10566254-s1",
      "quote": "類書『三才図会』によれば、大闍婆国（だいしゃばこく、ジャワ島のこと）に、頭を飛ばす者がいる。目に瞳が無いのが特徴で、現地では虫落（むしおとし）、落民（らくみん、首が落ちる人の意）と呼ばれる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sancai Tuhui menggambarkan manusia penerbang kepala di Jawa dengan mata tanpa pupil.",
        "en": "Sancai Tuhui describes head-flying people in Java with eyes lacking pupils."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10566254-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q10566254-s1",
      "quote": "唐代の書『南方異物誌』によれば、嶺南（中国南部からベトナムにかけての地方）の洞穴の中にいる飛頭蛮は、首に赤い傷跡があることが特徴で、夜には耳を翼のように使って飛び回り、虫を食べ、夜が明けると元の体に戻ってくるという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah selatan memberi mereka tanda merah di leher, telinga mirip sayap, dan kepulangan pada fajar.",
        "en": "A southern account gives them red neck marks, winglike ears and a dawn return."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10566254-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q10566254-s1",
      "quote": "頭部の離れた体を見ると、体が冷たくなっていた上に呼吸も微かになっていたので、布団をかけたところ、やがて戻ってきた首が布団に遮られて胴に戻ることができず、呼吸を荒らげて苦しみだし、布団を取り去ると首が胴に戻って落ち着いたという。また、銅の盆で胴体を覆った人もいたが、その際には首が胴に戻れず、とうとう死んでしまったという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah menyebut penutupan tubuh dapat menghalangi kembalinya kepala dan menyebabkan kematian.",
        "en": "Stories say covering the body can obstruct the head’s return and cause death."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Teks Sekien membandingkan api gaib medan perang dengan penampakan lentera yang belum dikenalnya. Kisah Yamagata belakangan tentang lentera tua yang menyala sendiri berasal dari buku yang dikenal memuat cerita rekaan.",

    {
      "id": "wd-q10913525-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E5%8F%A4%E7%B1%A0%E7%81%AB",
      "title": "古籠火",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q10913525-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q10913525-s1",
      "quote": "古籠火（ころうか）は、鳥山石燕の『百器徒然袋』にある日本の妖怪。 石灯籠の上に座り口から火を吐く姿で描かれている。灯籠の火の妖怪として石燕が描いたものであると考えられている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Korōka adalah yōkai api lentera Sekien, digambar duduk di lentera batu dan menyemburkan api.",
        "en": "Korōka is Sekien’s lantern-fire yōkai, shown seated on a stone lantern and breathing fire."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10913525-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q10913525-s1",
      "quote": "「古戦場には汗血（かんけつ）のこりて鬼火となり、あやしきかたちをあらはすよしを聞（きき）はべれどもいまだ灯籠の火の怪をなすことをきかずと」と石燕は記しており、特に典拠とした古文献はないようである。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teks Sekien membandingkan api gaib medan perang dengan penampakan lentera yang belum dikenalnya.",
        "en": "Sekien’s text contrasts battlefield ghost fires with his unfamiliar lantern apparition."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10913525-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q10913525-s1",
      "quote": "老いた奉公人が言うには、あれは古籠火というもので、古びた灯籠がしばらく火を入れてもらえないと、ひとりでに火が灯るのだという。ただし、この話の出典である『東北怪談の旅』は、山田によって創作された本来存在しない妖怪伝承が多数収録されていることで有名である。 また、この話は、水木しげるの著作における古籠火（ころうび）の解説でも引用されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah Yamagata belakangan tentang lentera tua yang menyala sendiri berasal dari buku yang dikenal memuat cerita rekaan.",
        "en": "A later Yamagata story about a self-lighting old lantern comes from a book known for invented tales."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "images",

    }
  ]
}

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q10917486-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q10917486-s1",
      "quote": "蚊帳吊り狸（かやつりたぬき、かやつりだぬき）は、徳島県美馬市（旧美馬郡三島村舞中島）に伝わる妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kayatsuri-tanuki adalah yōkai dari Mima di Tokushima.",
        "en": "Kayatsuri-tanuki is a yōkai of Mima in Tokushima."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10917486-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q10917486-s1",
      "quote": "寂しい夜道を人が歩いていると、道の真ん中に蚊帳が吊ってある。室内に吊る筈の蚊帳がなぜこんな野外に、と奇異に思いつつも通り抜けないと先へ進めないので、蚊帳をまくり上げてみると、その中にはまた蚊帳が吊ってある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pelancong menemukan kelambu berlapis yang menghalangi jalan malam sunyi.",
        "en": "Travelers find nested mosquito nets blocking a lonely nighttime road."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10917486-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q10917486-s1",
      "quote": "その蚊帳をまくると中にはさらに蚊帳が……と繰り返している内に、前にも後ろにも蚊帳がある状態となり、先へ進むどころかもとの場所へ戻ることもできず、一晩中その場で右往左往する羽目になってしまう。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kelambu berulang menjebak pelancong tanpa jalan maju atau kembali.",
        "en": "Repeated nets trap the traveler without a way forward or back."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10917486-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q10917486-s1",
      "quote": "この怪異に遭遇した際には決して慌てることなく、心を平静に保ち、丹田に力を込めて蚊帳をまくっていくと、36枚目の蚊帳をまくったときに外に出られるという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda menyarankan ketenangan dan kegigihan hingga kelambu ketiga puluh enam membuka jalan.",
        "en": "The legend advises calmness and persistence until the thirty-sixth net opens the way."
      }
    }

      "en": "Kimitezuri is a goddess in Ryukyuan Shinto. Tradition links her to sea and sun and possession of the chief priestess during a king’s accession.",

        "wd-q10918482-c02"

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q10918482-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q10918482-s1",
      "quote": "君手摩（きみてずり）または君手（きみて）、君てっ（きみてっ、きみって）は、琉球神道に伝わる女神。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kimitezuri adalah dewi dalam Shinto Ryukyu.",
        "en": "Kimitezuri is a goddess in Ryukyuan Shinto."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10918482-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q10918482-s1",
      "quote": "通説では海と太陽を司る琉球王国の守護神とされており、琉球王国の存亡の機に降臨すると言われる。ニライカナイ（海の深淵、海の彼方、地下にある）に住まうとされており、新しい国王の即位の儀式中、聞得大君に憑依するとされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi mengaitkannya dengan laut serta matahari dan perasukan pendeta utama selama penobatan raja.",
        "en": "Tradition links her to sea and sun and possession of the chief priestess during a king’s accession."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10918482-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q10918482-s1",
      "quote": "君手摩を神の名とする初出史料は向象賢（羽地朝秀）が編纂した『中山世鑑』である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Chūzan Seikan disebut sebagai sumber pertama yang memperlakukan istilah itu sebagai nama dewa.",
        "en": "Chūzan Seikan is cited as the first source treating the term as a divine name."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10918482-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q10918482-s1",
      "quote": "しかし、一般に「君」は琉球の祭祀をつかさどる祝女（ノロ）を意味し、「手摩」は祈祷の際に手をすり合わせることを意味すること、また君手摩を行事として記載する別の史料もあることから、君手摩は神名ではなく、宗教儀式名であると解釈する説もある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tafsir lain memperlakukan Kimitezuri sebagai nama ritual alih-alih nama dewa.",

      }
    }

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Chirizuka Kaiō adalah yōkai yang digambar Sekien. Ia membuka peti dalam komposisi yang terkait dengan ilustrasi arak-arakan iblis lebih tua. Namanya terkait dengan bagian tumpukan debu Tsurezuregusa bersama Fuguruma Yōhi.",
      "en": "Chirizuka Kaiō is a yōkai illustrated by Sekien. It opens a chest in a composition linked to older illustrated demon parades. Its name is linked with Tsurezuregusa’s dust-heap passage alongside Fuguruma Yōhi.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q10931176-c01",
        "wd-q10931176-c02",
        "wd-q10931176-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sekien menyebutnya pemimpin yamauba melalui permainan kata sastra, tanpa legenda penghubung yang diketahui. Uraian belakangan memberinya wujud oni kecil merah berbulu dan pakaian lusuh. Tafsir belakangan menjadikannya raja tsukumogami alih-alih yamauba.",
      "en": "Sekien calls it a leader of yamauba through literary wordplay, without a known connecting legend. The later description gives it a small red hairy oni’s form and worn clothes. Later interpretation makes it a king of tsukumogami instead of yamauba.",

      "id": "wd-q10931176-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q10931176-s1",
      "quote": "『百器徒然袋』には、唐櫃（からと、からびつ）をこじ開ける姿が描かれている。室町時代から江戸時代にかけて描かれている『百鬼夜行絵巻』にも同じく唐櫃をこじ開ける赤い鬼の構図があり、それが参考になっていると考えられる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia membuka peti dalam komposisi yang terkait dengan ilustrasi arak-arakan iblis lebih tua.",
        "en": "It opens a chest in a composition linked to older illustrated demon parades."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10931176-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q10931176-s1",
      "quote": "『徒然草』第72段に「多くて見苦しからぬは、文車の文、塵塚の塵」という文があり、『百器徒然袋』には『百鬼夜行絵巻』や『徒然草』からの引用が多く見てとれることから、この塵塚怪王もそれらを元に石燕が創作したものといわれる。また前述の「文車の文」の箇所からは「文車妖妃」が創作されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya terkait dengan bagian tumpukan debu Tsurezuregusa bersama Fuguruma Yōhi.",
        "en": "Its name is linked with Tsurezuregusa’s dust-heap passage alongside Fuguruma Yōhi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10931176-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q10931176-s1",
      "quote": "石燕は「ちりづか怪王はちりつもりてなれる山姥とうの長なるべしと」と書いており山姥などの王であるとしているが、塵と山姥については能の『山姥』の「妄執の雲の塵積って山姥となれる」という詞章を引いた表現であり、山姥と塵塚怪王（または『百鬼夜行絵巻』の赤い鬼）との関連が見られる伝承や物語なども確認されておらず、明確な立ち位置は良く分かっていない。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sekien menyebutnya pemimpin yamauba melalui permainan kata sastra, tanpa legenda penghubung yang diketahui.",
        "en": "Sekien calls it a leader of yamauba through literary wordplay, without a known connecting legend."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10931176-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q10931176-s2",
      "quote": "Chirizuka kaiō is a red, hairy demon who resembles a small oni. His clothing is old and tattered.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian belakangan memberinya wujud oni kecil merah berbulu dan pakaian lusuh.",
        "en": "The later description gives it a small red hairy oni’s form and worn clothes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10931176-c06",
      "source_id": "wd-q10931176-s2",
      "quote": "Despite this phrasing, chirizuka kaiō has come to be interpreted as the king of tsukumogami rather than yama uba.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tafsir belakangan menjadikannya raja tsukumogami alih-alih yamauba.",
        "en": "Later interpretation makes it a king of tsukumogami instead of yamauba."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
    {
      "topic": {
        "id": "Makna pemimpin / Meaning of leadership",
        "en": "Makna pemimpin / Meaning of leadership"
      },
      "positions": [
        {
          "summary": {
            "id": "Permainan kata Sekien menyebut pemimpin yamauba.",
            "en": "Sekien’s wordplay mentions leadership of yamauba."
          },
          "claim_ids": [
            "wd-q10931176-c04"
          ]
        },
        {
          "summary": {
            "id": "Tafsir belakangan menyebut raja tsukumogami.",
            "en": "Later interpretation makes it king of tsukumogami."
          },
          "claim_ids": [

          ]

      ]

  ],

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Tenjōname adalah yōkai Sekien berlidah panjang yang menjilat langit-langit.",
    "en": "Tenjōname is Sekien’s yōkai with a long tongue licking the ceiling.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q10939056-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Tenjōname adalah yōkai Sekien berlidah panjang yang menjilat langit-langit. Sekien menghubungkannya dengan bagian tentang langit-langit tinggi yang membuat musim dingin dingin dan cahaya lampu redup. Peneliti mengaitkan desainnya dengan ilustrasi yōkai lebih tua.",
      "en": "Tenjōname is Sekien’s yōkai with a long tongue licking the ceiling. Sekien connects it with a passage about high ceilings making winter cold and lamplight dim. Researchers relate its design to an older illustrated yōkai.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q10939056-c01",
        "wd-q10939056-c02",
        "wd-q10939056-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Panduan modern menambahkan noda langit-langit kotor dan tanda berbentuk wajah menakutkan. Ensiklopedia belakangan juga menunjuk Sekien dan citra gulungan lebih tua. Uraian itu membedakan desain abad kedelapan belas dari dongeng yang kemudian diciptakan.",
      "en": "Modern guides add dirty ceiling stains and frightening face-shaped marks. The later encyclopedia likewise points to Sekien and older scroll imagery. That account distinguishes the eighteenth-century design from subsequently invented tales.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q10939056-c04",
        "wd-q10939056-c05",
        "wd-q10939056-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],
  "relations": [],

  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q10939056-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E5%A4%A9%E4%BA%95%E5%98%97",
      "title": "天井嘗",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",

      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10939056-s2",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://yokai.com/tenjouname/",
      "title": "Tenjōname",
      "publisher": "Yokai.com",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "author": "Matthew Meyer"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q10939056-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q10939056-s1",
      "quote": "天井嘗（てんじょうなめ）は、鳥山石燕の妖怪画集『百器徒然袋』にある日本の妖怪である。 == 概要 == 長い舌で天井をなめる姿で描かれている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tenjōname adalah yōkai Sekien berlidah panjang yang menjilat langit-langit.",
        "en": "Tenjōname is Sekien’s yōkai with a long tongue licking the ceiling."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10939056-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q10939056-s1",
      "quote": "『百器徒然袋』は『徒然草』の文を多く題材としており、この妖怪にも『徒然草』第五十五段にある「天井の高きは冬寒く燈（ともしび）暗し」という文が石燕による解説文中に引用されている。石燕は「冬寒く燈暗し」という状況は天井嘗が発生させているとしている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sekien menghubungkannya dengan bagian tentang langit-langit tinggi yang membuat musim dingin dingin dan cahaya lampu redup.",
        "en": "Sekien connects it with a passage about high ceilings making winter cold and lamplight dim."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10939056-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q10939056-s1",
      "quote": "『百鬼徒然袋』の他の妖怪と同様に、そのデザインは室町時代の『百鬼夜行絵巻』にある妖怪をモデルとして描かれており、顔を仰向けて舌を出した妖怪がそれにあたるものであると考えられている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Peneliti mengaitkan desainnya dengan ilustrasi yōkai lebih tua.",
        "en": "Researchers relate its design to an older illustrated yōkai."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10939056-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q10939056-s1",
      "quote": "昭和・平成の妖怪関連の書籍では、長い舌をつかってほこりの集った天井をなめる妖怪であり、天井の板などに発生する「しみ」はこのなめ跡であると解説されている。天井以外にも柱や壁もなめるとも、天井嘗によってつけられたしみは、ときには化け物や恐ろしい表情の人間の顔に見えるため、寝床でそれを見上げている人は恐怖に駆られ、ついには発狂して死んだ者もいた述べられていることもある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Panduan modern menambahkan noda langit-langit kotor dan tanda berbentuk wajah menakutkan.",
        "en": "Modern guides add dirty ceiling stains and frightening face-shaped marks."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10939056-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q10939056-s2",
      "quote": "Tenjōname first appears in Toriyama Sekien’s Hyakki tsurezure bukuro , although its appearance seems to be inspired by older yōkai scrolls.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ensiklopedia belakangan juga menunjuk Sekien dan citra gulungan lebih tua.",
        "en": "The later encyclopedia likewise points to Sekien and older scroll imagery."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10939056-c06",
      "source_id": "wd-q10939056-s2",
      "quote": "Since tenjōname was created in the 18th century, older folktales about it do not exist. However, since then a number of stories have been invented.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian itu membedakan desain abad kedelapan belas dari dongeng yang kemudian diciptakan.",
        "en": "That account distinguishes the eighteenth-century design from subsequently invented tales."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Nebutori adalah sosok gaib dalam Ehon Hyakumonogatari.",
    "en": "Nebutori is a supernatural figure in Ehon Hyakumonogatari.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q10955404-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Nebutori adalah sosok gaib dalam Ehon Hyakumonogatari. Teks ilustrasi menggambarkan perempuan tidur menjadi sangat besar dan mendengkur keras. Buku itu juga memperlakukannya sebagai penyakit atau peringatan terhadap tidur berlebihan.",
      "en": "Nebutori is a supernatural figure in Ehon Hyakumonogatari. The illustration text describes a sleeping woman becoming enormous and snoring loudly. The book also treats it as an affliction or warning against oversleeping.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q10955404-c01",
        "wd-q10955404-c02",
        "wd-q10955404-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Karya belakangan menambahkan tanuki perasuk sebagai satu penjelasan. Penuturan belakangan mengulang gambaran tujuh futon baginya dan tiga bagi suaminya. Uraian belakangan juga mengaitkannya dengan perasukan tanuki atau kitsune.",
      "en": "Later works add a possessing tanuki as one explanation. The later telling repeats the image of seven futons for her and three for her husband. The later account also attributes it to possession by tanuki or kitsune.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q10955404-c04",
        "wd-q10955404-c05",
        "wd-q10955404-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],
  "relations": [],
  "modern_depictions": [],

  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q10955404-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E5%AF%9D%E8%82%A5",
      "title": "寝肥",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10955404-s2",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://yokai.com/nebutori/",
      "title": "Nebutori",
      "publisher": "Yokai.com",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "author": "Matthew Meyer"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q10955404-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q10955404-s1",
      "quote": "寝肥、寝惚堕（ねぶとり）は、江戸時代の奇談集『絵本百物語』にある日本の妖怪の一種。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nebutori adalah sosok gaib dalam Ehon Hyakumonogatari.",
        "en": "Nebutori is a supernatural figure in Ehon Hyakumonogatari."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10955404-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q10955404-s1",
      "quote": "『絵本百物語』の挿絵中にある文章によれば、夜に女性が寝床につくと部屋に入りきらなくなるほどの巨体となり、雷鳴のような、車の轟くほど大きないびきをかいて寝るものを、寝肥というとある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teks ilustrasi menggambarkan perempuan tidur menjadi sangat besar dan mendengkur keras.",
        "en": "The illustration text describes a sleeping woman becoming enormous and snoring loudly."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10955404-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q10955404-s1",
      "quote": "また、『絵本百物語』の本文によれば、寝肥は女性の病気の一つであり、寝坊を戒めた言葉ともされる。奥州（現・青森県・岩手県）で寝肥となった女性が、家に布団が10枚あるところを、その女性は7枚、夫は3枚使って寝ていたという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Buku itu juga memperlakukannya sebagai penyakit atau peringatan terhadap tidur berlebihan.",
        "en": "The book also treats it as an affliction or warning against oversleeping."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10955404-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q10955404-s1",
      "quote": "『視聴草』『兎園小説拾遺』などの江戸時代の書物には、老女の体にタヌキが入り込み、途端に老女が元気になって大量の食物を平らげるようになったという奇談があることから（狸憑き#怪奇譚を参照）、寝肥もまた、眠っている女性の体にタヌキが忍び込んで悪さをしているのではないかという解説が昭和以降の妖怪に関する文献ではされることもある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karya belakangan menambahkan tanuki perasuk sebagai satu penjelasan.",
        "en": "Later works add a possessing tanuki as one explanation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10955404-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q10955404-s2",
      "quote": "If a home has ten futons, a woman with nebutori will take up seven of them and leave only three for her husband.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penuturan belakangan mengulang gambaran tujuh futon baginya dan tiga bagi suaminya.",
        "en": "The later telling repeats the image of seven futons for her and three for her husband."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q10955404-c06",
      "source_id": "wd-q10955404-s2",
      "quote": "Nebutori is sometimes blamed on tanuki or kitsune. Both animals can possess humans and cause them to do strange things.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian belakangan juga mengaitkannya dengan perasukan tanuki atau kitsune.",
        "en": "The later account also attributes it to possession by tanuki or kitsune."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Mokugyo Daruma adalah yōkai gong ikan kayu dalam kumpulan ilustrasi Sekien.",
    "en": "Mokugyo Daruma is a wooden-fish-gong yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated collection.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q11093248-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Mokugyo Daruma adalah yōkai gong ikan kayu dalam kumpulan ilustrasi Sekien. Ia berwajah berjanggut mirip Daruma dan duduk pada bantal bundar. Satu tafsir menghubungkan alat musik dan Bodhidharma melalui tradisi latihan tanpa tidur.",
      "en": "Mokugyo Daruma is a wooden-fish-gong yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated collection. It has a bearded Daruma-like face and sits on a round cushion. An interpretation links the instrument and Bodhidharma through traditions of wakeful practice.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q11093248-c01",
        "wd-q11093248-c02",
        "wd-q11093248-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Cetakan Yoshitoshi tahun 1865 dianggap mengikuti desain Sekien. Uraian belakangan menghubungkan gong kayu dengan irama sutra dan keadaan terjaga. Ia menafsirkan yōkai melalui simbolisme bersama tentang keadaan terjaga.",
      "en": "Yoshitoshi’s 1865 print is considered to follow Sekien’s design. The later account links the wooden gongs with sutra rhythm and wakefulness. It interprets the yōkai through shared symbolism of wakefulness.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q11093248-c04",
        "wd-q11093248-c05",
        "wd-q11093248-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "etymology": null,

    {

      "title": "木魚達磨",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",

      "author": null
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11093248-s2",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://yokai.com/mokugyodaruma/",
      "title": "Mokugyo daruma",
      "publisher": "Yokai.com",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "author": "Matthew Meyer"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q11093248-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q11093248-s1",
      "quote": "木魚達磨（もくぎょだるま）は、鳥山石燕による妖怪画集『百器徒然袋』にある日本の妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mokugyo Daruma adalah yōkai gong ikan kayu dalam kumpulan ilustrasi Sekien.",
        "en": "Mokugyo Daruma is a wooden-fish-gong yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated collection."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11093248-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q11093248-s1",
      "quote": "だるまのようなひげの生えた顔をして円座にのっている木魚（もくぎょ）の姿で描かれており、石燕による解説文では木魚と同じ仏具の妖怪である払子守の同類（「払子守とおなじきものかと」とある）であると説明されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia berwajah berjanggut mirip Daruma dan duduk pada bantal bundar.",
        "en": "It has a bearded Daruma-like face and sits on a round cushion."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11093248-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q11093248-s1",
      "quote": "木魚は本来、魚が昼夜問わず目を開けたままであることから、修行僧に対して不眠不休の修行を説くために作られたものであり、また、達摩大師（だるまだいし）も、眠らずに9年間修行したと伝えられてことから、眠らない物の連想として石燕が創作した妖怪であると考えられている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu tafsir menghubungkan alat musik dan Bodhidharma melalui tradisi latihan tanpa tidur.",
        "en": "An interpretation links the instrument and Bodhidharma through traditions of wakeful practice."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11093248-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q11093248-s1",
      "quote": "浮世絵師・月岡芳年は錦絵『百器夜行』（1865年）に石燕の木魚達摩を参考にしたと見られる絵を描いている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cetakan Yoshitoshi tahun 1865 dianggap mengikuti desain Sekien.",
        "en": "Yoshitoshi’s 1865 print is considered to follow Sekien’s design."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11093248-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q11093248-s2",
      "quote": "They are used to keep the rhythm when chanting sutras. They are also used to keep people awake during meditation.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian belakangan menghubungkan gong kayu dengan irama sutra dan keadaan terjaga.",
        "en": "The later account links the wooden gongs with sutra rhythm and wakefulness."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11093248-c06",
      "source_id": "wd-q11093248-s2",
      "quote": "Due to their shared symbolism, Sekien combined these two figures of wakefulness into one tsukumogami.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menafsirkan yōkai melalui simbolisme bersama tentang keadaan terjaga.",
        "en": "It interprets the yōkai through shared symbolism of wakefulness."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Belum ada berkas Wikimedia Commons yang diperiksa untuk entri ini."
    }

}

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Kutsutsura adalah yōkai terkait sepatu dalam kumpulan ilustrasi Sekien.",
    "en": "Kutsutsura is a shoe-related yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated collection.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q11139313-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kutsutsura adalah yōkai terkait sepatu dalam kumpulan ilustrasi Sekien. Sekien menghubungkannya dengan cerita makhluk ladang melon Tiongkok yang rinciannya tetap tidak pasti. Pasangannya dengan Naga-kaburi mengacu pada peribahasa sepatu di ladang melon dan topi di bawah pohon plum.",
      "en": "Kutsutsura is a shoe-related yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated collection. Sekien connects it with a Chinese melon-field creature story whose details remain uncertain. Its pairing with Naga-kaburi echoes a proverb about shoes in melon fields and hats beneath plum trees.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q11139313-c01",
        "wd-q11139313-c02",
        "wd-q11139313-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Peneliti menghubungkan wujudnya dengan hewan pembawa sepatu dalam citra gulungan lebih tua. Uraian belakangan memberi wujud manusia dan hewan, dengan bangsawan memakai sepatu sebagai topi. Penggambaran hewannya bulat dan berbulu dengan moncong mirip sepatu bot.",
      "en": "Researchers connect its shape with a shoe-bearing beast in older scroll imagery. The later account gives human and animal forms, with a noble wearing a shoe as a hat. Its animal depiction is round and hairy with a bootlike snout.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q11139313-c04",
        "wd-q11139313-c05",
        "wd-q11139313-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q11139313-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q11139313-s1",
      "quote": "沓頬（くつつら）は、鳥山石燕による妖怪画集『百器徒然袋』に描かれている日本の妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kutsutsura adalah yōkai terkait sepatu dalam kumpulan ilustrasi Sekien.",
        "en": "Kutsutsura is a shoe-related yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated collection."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11139313-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q11139313-s1",
      "quote": "ウリの畑に現われてウリを食べてしまう妖怪がいたが、寺からもらった霊符（中身は「李下不正冠」と書いてあるだけだった）を置いておいたところ、その妖怪現われなくなったという中国の妖怪についての話が内容としては書かれており、この沓頬もそれであろうと石燕は述べている。ウリ畑にあらわれたというこの中国の妖怪の説話および詳細は不明である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sekien menghubungkannya dengan cerita makhluk ladang melon Tiongkok yang rinciannya tetap tidak pasti.",
        "en": "Sekien connects it with a Chinese melon-field creature story whose details remain uncertain."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11139313-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q11139313-s1",
      "quote": "石燕は長冠とおなじ見開きに沓頬を描いており、この2体は中国のことわざ「瓜田に履（くつ）を入れず李下に冠（かんむり）を正さず」および、『徒然草』に冠（65段）と沓（66段）が登場することをモチーフにして創作されていると考えられている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pasangannya dengan Naga-kaburi mengacu pada peribahasa sepatu di ladang melon dan topi di bawah pohon plum.",
        "en": "Its pairing with Naga-kaburi echoes a proverb about shoes in melon fields and hats beneath plum trees."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11139313-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q11139313-s1",
      "quote": "また、室町時代の妖怪絵巻『百鬼夜行絵巻』に描かれている浅沓（あさぐつ）をのせたヤマアラシのような形の妖怪がモデルになって描かれたと考えられている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Peneliti menghubungkan wujudnya dengan hewan pembawa sepatu dalam citra gulungan lebih tua.",
        "en": "Researchers connect its shape with a shoe-bearing beast in older scroll imagery."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11139313-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q11139313-s2",
      "quote": "They can take either a human form or an animal form. In human form, they look like a court noble wearing a shoe as a hat.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian belakangan memberi wujud manusia dan hewan, dengan bangsawan memakai sepatu sebagai topi.",
        "en": "The later account gives human and animal forms, with a noble wearing a shoe as a hat."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11139313-c06",
      "source_id": "wd-q11139313-s2",
      "quote": "In animal form, they appear as a round, hairy, beast with a fur boot for a snout.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggambaran hewannya bulat dan berbulu dengan moncong mirip sepatu bot.",
        "en": "Its animal depiction is round and hairy with a bootlike snout."
      }
    }
  ],

    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Zhu adalah burung pertanda buruk mirip burung hantu dengan tangan manusia dalam Kitab Gunung dan Laut. Bagian pegunungan selatan menempatkannya di Gunung Ju.",
      "en": "Zhu is an ominous owl-like bird with human hands in the Classic of Mountains and Seas. The southern-mountains section places it at Mount Ju.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q11173930-c01",
        "wd-q11173930-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Suaranya menyerupai puyuh betina dan penampakannya meramalkan pengasingan pejabat. Pembacaan harfiah membedakan burung bertangan manusia dari burung berwajah manusia.",
      "en": "Its call resembles a female quail and its sighting portends officials’ exile. A literal reading distinguishes a human-handed bird from a human-faced bird.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q11173930-c03",
        "wd-q11173930-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q11173930-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q11173930-s1",
      "quote": "鴸（しゅ）は、『山海経』「南山経」に記載される怪鳥。梟に似た人間の手を持つ鳥で、鴸が現れた場所では多くの官吏が追放されるという不吉な鳥。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Zhu adalah burung pertanda buruk mirip burung hantu dengan tangan manusia dalam Kitab Gunung dan Laut.",
        "en": "Zhu is an ominous owl-like bird with human hands in the Classic of Mountains and Seas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11173930-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q11173930-s1",
      "quote": "形状は鴟（フクロウ）に似て、人の手をもつ怪鳥が、柜山（くさん）（「ヤナギ」の意の山）に棲んでいると『南山経』に記される。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bagian pegunungan selatan menempatkannya di Gunung Ju.",
        "en": "The southern-mountains section places it at Mount Ju."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11173930-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q11173930-s1",
      "quote": "その鳴き声は痺（ウズラの雌）に似ており、その鴸と言う名称も、鳴き声にちなむ。人の目にふれることあれば、それはその県で多くの放士（官吏の放逐）がされる予兆だとしている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Suaranya menyerupai puyuh betina dan penampakannya meramalkan pengasingan pejabat.",
        "en": "Its call resembles a female quail and its sighting portends officials’ exile."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11173930-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q11173930-s1",
      "quote": "あくまで『山海経』じたいの文にしたがうならば、人の手を持つ鳥には属すが、人面鳥には属さない。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pembacaan harfiah membedakan burung bertangan manusia dari burung berwajah manusia.",
        "en": "A literal reading distinguishes a human-handed bird from a human-faced bird."
      }
    }

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Naga putih adalah naga Tiongkok yang secara tradisional melayani kaisar langit. Kisah memberinya penerbangan sangat cepat dan perubahan sesekali menjadi ikan.",
      "en": "The white dragon is a Chinese dragon traditionally serving the celestial emperor. Accounts give it especially fast flight and occasional transformations into a fish.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q11244093-c01",
        "wd-q11244093-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Novel Jepang memberi penerbangannya cahaya serta gelombang dan bahan yang dimuntahkannya efek pembentuk emas. Versi lain mengaitkan naga putih dengan perlindungan barat melalui simbolisme Lima Unsur.",
      "en": "A Japanese novel gives its flight light and waves and its expelled material a gold-making effect. A variant associates the white dragon with western protection through Five Elements symbolism.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q11244093-c03",
        "wd-q11244093-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],

  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q11244093-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E7%99%BD%E7%AB%9C",
      "title": "白竜",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q11244093-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q11244093-s1",
      "quote": "白竜（はくりゅう、白龍、拼音: báilóng パイロン）は、古代中国で、天上界の皇帝である天帝に仕えているとされた竜の一種。名前のとおり、全身の鱗が白い。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Naga putih adalah naga Tiongkok yang secara tradisional melayani kaisar langit.",
        "en": "The white dragon is a Chinese dragon traditionally serving the celestial emperor."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11244093-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q11244093-s1",
      "quote": "竜は基本的に空を飛べるが、白竜は特に空を飛ぶ速度が速く、これに乗っていれば他の竜に追いつかれないともいう。ときおり魚に化けて地上の泉などで泳いでいることもある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah memberinya penerbangan sangat cepat dan perubahan sesekali menjadi ikan.",
        "en": "Accounts give it especially fast flight and occasional transformations into a fish."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11244093-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q11244093-s1",
      "quote": "曲亭馬琴の読本『南総里見八犬伝』にも登場し、白竜が光を放ち、波を巻きたてながら南の方へ飛んで行くという場面があり、白竜が物を吐くとそれが地面に入って金になるという解説がついている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Novel Jepang memberi penerbangannya cahaya serta gelombang dan bahan yang dimuntahkannya efek pembentuk emas.",
        "en": "A Japanese novel gives its flight light and waves and its expelled material a gold-making effect."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11244093-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q11244093-s1",
      "quote": "五行思想においては、白は西を意味するため、白竜を白虎と同様、西方を守護する神聖な竜とする異説がある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi lain mengaitkan naga putih dengan perlindungan barat melalui simbolisme Lima Unsur.",
        "en": "A variant associates the white dragon with western protection through Five Elements symbolism."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Tsuitate-danuki adalah tanuki pengubah wujud dari Wakimachi di Tokushima.",
    "en": "Tsuitate-danuki is a transforming tanuki of Wakimachi in Tokushima.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q11255764-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Tsuitate-danuki adalah tanuki pengubah wujud dari Wakimachi di Tokushima. Ia menghalangi jalan malam sebagai sekat besar, tetapi pelancong yang teguh dapat melewatinya.",
      "en": "Tsuitate-danuki is a transforming tanuki of Wakimachi in Tokushima. It blocks a night road as a large screen, but a determined traveler can pass through.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q11255764-c01",
        "wd-q11255764-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Legenda menyebut pembacaan mantra bersama dan monumen batu menyegelnya. Laporan lapangan belakangan menyebut monumen penyegelan bertahan sampai dicuri pada zaman Shōwa.",
      "en": "The legend credits collective chanting and a stone monument with sealing it away. A later field report says the sealing monument survived until being stolen in the Shōwa period.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q11255764-c03",
        "wd-q11255764-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],

  "timeline": [],

  "modern_depictions": [],
  "tradition_vs_modern": null,

  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q11255764-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E8%A1%9D%E7%AB%8B%E7%8B%B8",
      "title": "衝立狸",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q11255764-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q11255764-s1",
      "quote": "衝立狸（ついたてだぬき）は、徳島県美馬郡脇町（現・美馬市）に伝わる化け狸。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tsuitate-danuki adalah tanuki pengubah wujud dari Wakimachi di Tokushima.",
        "en": "Tsuitate-danuki is a transforming tanuki of Wakimachi in Tokushima."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11255764-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q11255764-s1",
      "quote": "人が夜道を歩いていると、大きな衝立となって道の真ん中に現れる。大抵の人は足止めをされたことに驚いて引き返すが、強気な人が丹田（臍）に力を込め、構わずに突き進むと、そのまま通り抜けることができるという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menghalangi jalan malam sebagai sekat besar, tetapi pelancong yang teguh dapat melewatinya.",
        "en": "It blocks a night road as a large screen, but a determined traveler can pass through."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11255764-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q11255764-s1",
      "quote": "かつて多くの人々から恐れられ、この道を夜に通る人は殆どいなくなったため、付近の人々が集まって光明真言を4万8千遍唱え、緑泥片岩で高さ1丈程の石碑を建てて衝立狸を封印した（僧侶が封印したとの説もある）。以来、この怪異は無くなったという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda menyebut pembacaan mantra bersama dan monumen batu menyegelnya.",
        "en": "The legend credits collective chanting and a stone monument with sealing it away."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11255764-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q11255764-s1",
      "quote": "徳島の郷土史家・笠井新也による『阿波の狸の話』（1927/昭和2年）には、この石碑が現存するとあるものの、その後に妖怪研究家・村上健司が現地調査で土地の人々から取材したところによれば、衝立狸を封じたという石碑は近年まで高須に実在していたが、昭和40年代に心無い者による盗難に遭ってしまったため、現存していないという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Laporan lapangan belakangan menyebut monumen penyegelan bertahan sampai dicuri pada zaman Shōwa.",
        "en": "A later field report says the sealing monument survived until being stolen in the Shōwa period."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Sagari adalah yōkai kepala kuda dari Okayama yang menggantung pada pohon tepi jalan dan menakuti pelancong.",
    "en": "Sagari is a horse-head yōkai of Okayama that hangs from a roadside tree and frightens travelers.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q11267633-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Sagari adalah yōkai kepala kuda dari Okayama yang menggantung pada pohon tepi jalan dan menakuti pelancong. Kisah Kumamoto juga menghubungkan kepala kuda menggantung dengan demam setelah terlihat. Uraian belakangan juga menempatkan Sagari di Okayama dan Kumamoto.",
      "en": "Sagari is a horse-head yōkai of Okayama that hangs from a roadside tree and frightens travelers. Kumamoto accounts also connect hanging horse heads with fever after a sighting. The later account likewise places Sagari in Okayama and Kumamoto.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q11267633-c01",
        "wd-q11267633-c02",
        "wd-q11267633-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Uraian itu memberi roh kuda mati yang dibuang sebagai asalnya. Ia menyebut roh kuda dapat tersangkut pada pohon. Uraian belakangan mengaitkan teriakannya dengan demam yang ditakuti.",
      "en": "That account gives the spirits of discarded dead horses as their origin. It says horses’ souls can become caught in trees. The later account associates its cry with a feared fever.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q11267633-c04",
        "wd-q11267633-c05",
        "wd-q11267633-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "id": "wd-q11267633-s1",

    {
      "id": "wd-q11267633-s2",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://yokai.com/sagari/",
      "title": "Sagari",
      "publisher": "Yokai.com",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "author": "Matthew Meyer"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wd-q11267633-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q11267633-s1",
      "quote": "さがりは、岡山県邑久郡に伝わる妖怪。 == 概要 == 馬の首だけの姿をしており、路傍の古いエノキの木からぶら下がった状態で現れる。鳴き声をあげたりして、暗い夜道を歩いている人などを脅かすともいう。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sagari adalah yōkai kepala kuda dari Okayama yang menggantung pada pohon tepi jalan dan menakuti pelancong.",
        "en": "Sagari is a horse-head yōkai of Okayama that hangs from a roadside tree and frightens travelers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11267633-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q11267633-s1",
      "quote": "熊本県でも同様の怪異があり、南ノ関町大字関下字迎町（現・玉名郡南関町）では旧道のはずれにある柿の木から、玉名村大字玉名字岡（現・玉名市玉名字岡）では榎の大樹(岡公民館(現在移転）辺りにあった榎だと思われる）から馬の首がぶら下がることがあり，これを目にした者は熱病を患ってしまうとして、人々から恐れられていたという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah Kumamoto juga menghubungkan kepala kuda menggantung dengan demam setelah terlihat.",
        "en": "Kumamoto accounts also connect hanging horse heads with fever after a sighting."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11267633-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q11267633-s2",
      "quote": "Sagari is a strange apparition from West Japan and Kyushu, particularly Okayama and Kumamoto prefectures.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian belakangan juga menempatkan Sagari di Okayama dan Kumamoto.",
        "en": "The later account likewise places Sagari in Okayama and Kumamoto."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11267633-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q11267633-s2",
      "quote": "Sagari come from the spirits of horses which die on the road and are discarded and left to rot where they fall.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian itu memberi roh kuda mati yang dibuang sebagai asalnya.",
        "en": "That account gives the spirits of discarded dead horses as their origin."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11267633-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q11267633-s2",
      "quote": "The horses’ souls sometimes get caught in the trees as they rise from the bodies.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menyebut roh kuda dapat tersangkut pada pohon.",
        "en": "It says horses’ souls can become caught in trees."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11267633-c06",
      "source_id": "wd-q11267633-s2",
      "quote": "However, those who hear a sagari’s whinnying and screaming may be stricken with a terrible fever.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian belakangan mengaitkan teriakannya dengan demam yang ditakuti.",
        "en": "The later account associates its cry with a feared fever."
      }
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "wd-q11275142-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q11275142-s1",
      "quote": "野馬（のうま）は、島根県邑智郡日貫地方や石見地方に伝わる妖怪。 == 伝承 == 一つ目の妖怪で、夜道で1人歩きしている人間を見つけると、突然現れて襲い掛かり、その人を食べてしまうという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nouma adalah yōkai pemakan manusia bermata satu dari Shimane.",
        "en": "Nouma is a one-eyed man-eating yōkai of Shimane."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11275142-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q11275142-s1",
      "quote": "昔、邑智郡日貫村でとある夜、たたらで銑を作っている人が寝ていると女がやってきて上に覆いかぶさった。すると遠くで野馬が鳴き、たたらへと近づいてきて窓から覗いた。しかし女がいるのを見ると一目散に逃げていった。この女の正体はかなやごさんで、たたらの神だとされている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dongeng pandai besi menyebut Kanayago melindungi orang tidur dengan membuat Nouma lari.",
        "en": "An ironworking tale has Kanayago protect a sleeper by making Nouma flee."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q11275142-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q11275142-s1",
      "quote": "漫画家・水木しげるは、伝承地方に古い神楽などが残されていることを理由として、数ある妖怪の中でも、かなり古いものに属すると推測している。一つ目という以外、姿が良く分かっていない。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mizuki menduga usianya tua sambil mengakui wujudnya tetap tidak jelas.",
        "en": "Mizuki speculates on its antiquity while acknowledging that its form remains unclear."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Belum ada berkas Wikimedia Commons yang diperiksa untuk entri ini."

    }
  ]
}

  "long_description": [

      "id": "Akagantā adalah yōkai Okinawa yang menyerupai bayi berambut merah. Ia muncul dalam rumah tua untuk membalik bantal orang tidur atau menekan tubuh mereka.",
      "en": "Akagantā is an Okinawan yōkai resembling a red-haired baby. It appears in old houses to overturn sleepers’ pillows or press on their bodies.",

        "wd-q11282679-c02"
      ]

      "en": "Interpretations link it with Kijimunā or with Zashiki-warashi. Hino Iwao records a red-haired child yōkai under the name Akagantā Warabā.",

      "source_id": "wd-q11282679-s1",
      "quote": "アカガンターは、沖縄県に伝わる妖怪。 姿は赤い髪の赤ん坊のようで、赤い服を着ていることもある。",
      "locator": "Article text",

      "statement": {
        "id": "Akagantā adalah yōkai Okinawa yang menyerupai bayi berambut merah.",

    {

      "source_id": "wd-q11282679-s1",
      "quote": "古びた家の中に現れ、夜に広間で寝ている者の枕をひっくり返したり、寝ている者を押さえつけたりするという。",
      "locator": "Article text",

    {

      "source_id": "wd-q11282679-s1",
      "quote": "外観や習性にキジムナーとの共通点があることから、キジムナーの仲間、もしくはキジムナーの別名とする説もある。妖怪研究家・多田克己は著書において、座敷童子と同種の妖怪としている。",
      "locator": "Article text",

      "quote": "民俗学者・日野巌による『日本妖怪変化語彙』にはアカガンターワラバーの名で、赤い髪の童子の妖怪の記述がある。",

        "id": "Hino Iwao mencatat yōkai anak berambut merah bernama Akagantā Warabā.",
